3Q26Pre: 환율 하락에도 성장 흐름 유지 
매출액 1조 3,477억원(+7.2%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 5,957억원(-6.0%, OPM 
44.2%)로 컨센서스에 부합할 것으로 전망. 3분기 원달러 평균 환율은 1,423원으로 전분기 
평균 환율 대비 약 5.2% 하락에도 불구하고 성장 흐름 지속. 
2분기 노조 파업으로 인한 영향은 3분기 생산을 진행했으며 4분기 매출에 반영될 예정으로 
연간 실적에 미치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상. GSK로부터 인수한 록빌 공장도 안정적
으로 생산이 이루어지고 있으며 CAPA 여유분은 추가 수주를 진행 중으로 28~29년 이후 
완전 가동 전망. 
4Q, 6공장 건설과 폴리펩타이드 인수 
유상 증자 관련 투자 설명서 상 투자 계획에 따르면 6공장 투자는 26년부터 29년까지 진행 
될 예정으로 29년 가동 목표. 6공장은 5공장과 동일한 18만 리터 규모로 건설되며, 연내 투
자 집행을 시작함에 따라 건설 발표도 임박한 것으로 추정. 동사는 일정 수준 이상 기존 공
장 수주를 확보한 후 후속 공장 건설을 진행해오고 있어 non-binding 수주 계약의 본계약 
전환도 순차적으로 이루어질 것으로 전망. 
폴리펩타이드 인수도 현재 공개 매수를 진행 중에 있으며 연내 마무리될 것으로 예상. 
1H26 폴리펩타이드 매출은 2.37억 유로(+41.6%)로 GLP-1 agonist 등 펩타이드 의약품 
수요 증가에 따라 높은 성장 지속. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원으로 하향 
매수 의견을 유지하며 목표 주가는 기존 210만원에서 200만원으로 하향. 목표 주가 하향은 
유상 증자에 따른 EPS 희석에 기인. 글로벌 항체 CDMO 수요가 여전히 견조하며, 폴리펩
타이드 인수로 펩타이드로의 확장에 따른 추가적인 성장 기대. 주주 대상 유상 증자 영향과 
파업 우려 등이 있으나 노사 합의 시 주가 반등 전망.

주: K-IFRS 연결 기준 
자료: HD현대중공업, LS증권 리서치센터

HiMSEN 엔진 및 SMR 주기기 생산설비 대규모 증설 발표 
- 9/10 HD현대중공업은 총 1조 722억원 규모의 시설투자 2건을 공시 
① 대출력 발전엔진(4행정 중속엔진, HiMSEN엔진) 생산능력 확충에 8,336
억원 투입, 글로벌 전력수요 증가에 따른 엔진발전 시장 확대에 대응하는 목
적. 대출력 발전엔진 전용 공장 신설과 토지 취득, 건축물·기계설비 등 생산설
비 구축이 골자. 투자기간은 ‘26.09~’28.05(설계/인허가 ~’27.02, 공장건설 
~’28.05. ’28.06 상업운전개시, 28년 가동률 30%, 2030년 100% 도달) 
② SMR 주기기 제작 생산시설 구축에 2,386억원(자기자본 대비 2.55%) 투
입, SMR 주기기 제작사업 진입 및 수주 물량 증가에 대응. SMR 전용 공장 
및 설비 신설과 기존 공장 최적화를 병행, 투자기간은 ’26.10~’29.04. 
기다리던 증설 공시. 늘어난 Capa로 AI DC향 수혜 확대 기대 
- 25년 3.0GW(선박용 2.3GW + 육상용 0.7GW)였던 HiMSEN 엔진 생산 
Capa는 2030년 7.2GW(선박용 3.2GW + 육상용 4.0GW)로 확대. 그간 선
박용 중심이었던 엔진 사업의 포트폴리오를 발전용으로 본격 다각화하는 사
업 재편으로 해석 
- AI DC 신규 증설 확대 추세 속 계통 연결의 어려움과 CCGT, Aero -
derivative 대비 납기 및 부하상승시간, 모듈화 용이성 영역에서 장점을 가진 
발전용 중속엔진에 대한 수요 지속 기대(260908 당사 보고서 참조) 
- 지난 Corban Energy Group 향 계약 건(1,000MW, $ 674 mn)을 단순 
적용 시 금번 증설의 향후 매출액 기여규모는 연간 $ 2.8 bn에 달함. 엔진 시
장의 Seller-Market 기조에 따라 20%를 상회하는 높은 이익률이 지속될 것
으로 전망함에 따라 향후 유의미한 이익 성장 기대 
- HD현대마린솔루션 또한 LTSA 수주 저변 확대 측면에서 향후 이익 확장 가
능. HiMSEN엔진 고유 라이선스 보유에 따른 시너지로 그룹사 전반의 수혜 
기대

Document 217: Europe’s construction sector faces a period of weak growth

Source PDF: all report pdf/Europe’s construction sector faces a period of weak growth.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

Europe’s construction volumes will keep growing but remain
subdued. Infrastructure investment and Germany’s gradual
rebound are drivers, while higher ﬁnancing costs and
geopolitical risks cap momentum. Housing permits have
stabilised, signalling that a stronger residential recovery will
have to wait
Maurice van Sante
Europe's construction sector is showing signs of stabilising, but growth remains
modest
Low growth returns in 2026
We expect EU construction output to grow by a marginal 0.5% in

2026, following a 1.8% decline in 2024 and 2.3% growth in 2025.
More broadly, EU GDP has outpaced construction output in recent
years, showing that the construction sector has lagged the broader
economy.
The positive signals seen in early 2025 have since weakened. By
September, the EU construction conﬁdence indicator had stopped
improving and had fallen below its level a year earlier. Nevertheless,

### Page 2

the EU economy has remained surprisingly resilient despite higher
energy prices, rising interest rates and greater geopolitical
uncertainty. Increased defence and infrastructure spending,
together with Germany’s cyclical rebound, is partly oﬀsetting the
headwinds.
EU construction lags GDP growth
Volumes, Index 2018 Q2 = 100
Source: Oxford economics, ING Research
The building sector has declined, while infrastructure has grown
The building sector, comprising residential and non-residential

construction, remains a weak spot. Higher interest rates and
construction costs, combined with economic uncertainty, are
making households and businesses more cautious about investing
in new property. By contrast, some segments stand out more
positively. Specialised construction has beneﬁted from renovation
and sustainability projects, while infrastructure investment,
supported by EU funds and spending on energy and digital
networks, has provided an additional oﬀset.

### Page 3

Source: Eurostat, *forecasts ING Research
Following ﬁve consecutive years of contraction, Germany’s
construction sector is expected to grow again in 2026. Europe’s
largest construction market shrank by more than 10% between
2020 and 2025. We expect a modest recovery this year, with
growth gradually gaining momentum in 2027, supported by
housing and infrastructure.
In 2025, France’s construction output fell by 3.5%, followed by
quarter-on-quarter declines of 1.2% and 0.9% in the ﬁrst two

quarters of 2026. French sector conﬁdence weakened further, while
housing permits in August remained below their level a year earlier.
Combined with sluggish GDP growth, this points to a 2.5%
contraction in 2026.
Spain’s construction sector is still recovering. After losing more than
25% of its output between 2019 and 2022, the market began to
rebound in 2023. We forecast growth of 3% in 2026. Building
permits have risen sharply in recent years, although momentum is

easing. Contractor conﬁdence is easing from a very high level in
recent months. Even so, robust GDP growth should support
construction this year and next.
For the Netherlands, we expect construction output to stabilise in
2026 after a weak ﬁrst half, followed by modest growth in 2027.

### Page 4

Housing construction should gradually recover as the recent rise in
building permits feeds through into starts and completions,
although projects are often delayed and the recovery will remain
limited. Infrastructure remains an important driver.
Poland’s construction sector had a volatile start to 2026, with
output falling by 6.8% in the ﬁrst quarter due to the cold winter
before rebounding by 8% in the second. Building permits are rising
slightly, conﬁdence remains stable and robust GDP growth of 3.4%

should support demand. The resumption of EU funding is also
expected to support infrastructure investment. Nevertheless, we
expect construction output to remain ﬂat in 2026, largely because
of the weak ﬁrst quarter, followed by modest growth of 2% in 2027.
Input prices are marginally increasing
Higher energy prices are pushing up production costs for energy-
intensive materials such as concrete, cement and bricks. Even so,
material prices are generally rising only gradually. In September,

13% of EU producers planned to raise selling prices over the next
three months, compared with 11% in January, before the Iran war.
Passing higher input costs through the value chain is also
becoming harder. Governments, non-residential investors and
homebuyers already face higher interest rates, making further cost
increases more diﬃcult to absorb.
Contract terms may also pose a risk for contractors. Public price-
adjustment schemes are often too limited to absorb shocks, while

countries without them, such as Spain, face greater exposure.
Private contracts oﬀer even less protection, increasing the risk of
disputes, delays and halted projects.

### Page 5

Source: Eurostat, ING Research
Low water levels are delaying deliveries
Low water levels are creating upward cost pressure on building
materials. Many raw materials used in building materials, such as
sand, gravel and chemicals, are transported by inland waterways.
When river levels are low, barges can carry less cargo, which
increases transport costs and delays deliveries in the construction
supply chain. The impact has remained limited but is slowly

increasing. In September, 8% of EU construction companies
reported production constraints due to material shortages. This is
above the 5.7% in January and the 6.4% recorded during the low-
water period in September 2018.
Proﬁt conﬁdence rebounds, but energy risks return
Higher energy prices and the prospect of rising building-material
costs initially alarmed construction companies, which feared a
sharp hit to proﬁt margins in the ﬁrst quarter of 2026. As the Iran

war continued, however, gas prices rose less dramatically than
during the 2022 energy crisis. Conﬁdence recovered, and second-
quarter proﬁt expectations exceeded pre-war levels. This rebound
may prove excessive, as energy prices have since begun rising
again.

### Page 6

Source: RICS, ING Research
Residential growth stalls
Between summer 2024 and the end of 2025, the number of
building permits issued in the EU rose by approximately 15%. Since
then, however, permit issuance has broadly stabilised, suggesting
that momentum has faded in recent months. Several factors may
explain this development:
Higher interest rates make homes less aﬀordable, particularly
new builds, because buyers often have to ﬁnance payments
during construction.

The conﬂict in the Middle East has heightened geopolitical
uncertainty. This may make buyers more reluctant to purchase
a home, especially a new build, because completion can take
up to two years. The longer waiting period increases
uncertainty compared with buying an existing home.
Higher energy prices have increased the cost of energy-
intensive building materials, although the rise in overall
construction costs has so far remained modest.
One possible oﬀset is that buyers may prefer new homes because

they are generally more energy-eﬃcient than existing properties.
However, the stabilisation in permit issuance suggests that this
advantage is not strong enough to drive further growth.
The number of permits issued for new houses is an important
indicator of future volumes in the residential sector. Typically, it
takes between one and two years from the granting of a permit to
the completion of a new home.

### Page 7

Recovery of issued building permits slows
EU Building permits new dwellings, SA (index 2021=100
Germany follows the broader European recovery after a particularly
sharp fall in housing permits in 2021 and 2022. The government’s
‘housing construction turbo’ (Bau-Turbo) aims to speed up
planning and approvals in high-demand areas. Its impact, however,
will depend on local authorities, which may hesitate over loss of
control and legal uncertainty.
The permit data for France at the beginning of the year looked

promising. However, the second quarter was disappointing. France
expanded its zero-interest mortgage scheme (PTZ: Prêt à Taux Zéro)
for new-build homes in April 2025 to support ﬁrst-time buyers and
boost housing construction, but the building permit data suggests
the policy has yet to gain traction.
Dutch permits for new homes surged in the second quarter of 2026.
Statistics Netherlands has since paused publication of recent data,
saying the increase appears implausibly large and may reﬂect

legislative changes related to grid congestion. Limited grid capacity
continues to delay new connections and construction projects,
while nitrogen restrictions cause further setbacks. The
government's plan to reduce nitrogen emissions of peak polluters
and make way for other business activities is now under
parliamentary review.
Spanish housing permits nearly tripled between 2020 and 2024,
largely reﬂecting a rebound from the exceptionally low pre-

### Page 8

pandemic era base and a recovery in housing demand. Growth now
appears to have peaked, with permits falling by almost 2% in the
second quarter of 2026. The government’s PERTE programme, co-
ﬁnanced through European recovery funds, aims to speed up and
reduce the cost of housing construction through prefabrication and
modular methods, but its impact will take time.
Business case still diﬃcult
Housing shortages remain severe in many European cities, but new

housing projects are more diﬃcult to make commercially viable in
some countries than in others. Although permit issuance has
increased in many markets, construction costs remain high.
Builders and developers often cannot fully pass these costs on to
buyers, especially where existing homes are still cheaper than a few
years ago. As buyers can choose between new and existing homes,
relatively higher new-build prices are likely to push many towards
existing properties.

This is particularly true in France, Germany and Austria, where
existing-home prices have risen less than the European average. In
France, they remain even below their levels three years ago.
Builders and developers therefore have very limited (or no) scope to
pass on higher material costs, leaving many projects unproﬁtable
at current market prices. As a result, they are postponing
construction until conditions improve.

### Page 9

Source: Eurostat, ING Research
Infrastructure growth continues
EU infrastructure output grew by 2.2% in 2025. Growth slowed in
the ﬁrst seven months of 2026 to around 0.4% year-on-year. A
relatively cold, wet winter across many European regions
temporarily disrupted activity at infrastructure sites, though the
sector recovered some of the lost ground in the following months.
Highest growth in energy works
Infrastructure subsectors EU-19, Index 2021=100

Source: Euroconstruct, ING Research
The EU energy construction sector has been growing since 2022,
driven by investments in renewable energy and electricity grid
expansion. Growth is expected to remain strong as many countries
in the EU continue to upgrade power infrastructure to support the
energy transition and rising electricity demand.

Download
Railway construction is showing strong growth. Across the EU,
activity is being supported by both network renewals and major

cross-border projects. Examples include Rail Baltica, which will
connect the Baltic States with Poland, the Lyon–Turin rail link
between France and Italy and high-speed rail investment in Spain,
Portugal and Italy. Together, these projects illustrate the EU’s drive
to expand rail capacity.
Telecommunications construction has grown modestly in recent
years, supported by ﬁbre-optic expansion and continued
investment in 5G networks across the EU. Although coverage is

improving, progress varies widely between member states and
signiﬁcant investment is still needed to extend high-capacity
networks, particularly in rural and other lagging areas.
Roadworks, which account for around 30% of EU infrastructure
output, remain under pressure as many local and regional
authorities face budget constraints. Across the EU, public funding is
competing with other investment and spending priorities, limiting
the resources available for road construction and maintenance.

Overall, Europe’s construction recovery will remain slow and uneven.
Infrastructure investment and a gradual German recovery will
provide support, but high ﬁnancing costs, weak housing economics
and geopolitical risks will limit momentum. For now, infrastructure
will do most of the heavy lifting within the sector.

Are investors rational?
The stock market strength and resilience over the last 3 months appears to be a rational response to strong
earnings and only limited signs that higher energy prices are materially affecting broad corporate profits.
Our dbLumina gauge indicates investors have been irrational during market

drops and periods of geopolitical tensions What does it mean to be ‘rational’?
Risk-off periods:
The tariff fears in 2025 and then the Iran
war in 2026 were key buying opportunities
in equity markets. Yet, the overwhelming
fear exhibited by investors in these periods
largely meant they sold off risk assets in an
emotional and irrational response.
Risk-on periods:
It can be rational to buy into a rising
market. Our indicator suggests this is what
happened in Q3 as earnings growth

continued to be strong.
Fear Indicators:
Sharp, disproportionate reactions to
threats, widespread sell-offs on
announcements, panic, flight to safety
beyond what might be fundamentally
warranted.

are less
rational

Emotions cooled as ‘rational’ investing took over
Lower realised volatility in markets in Q3 helped clam emotional market responses. That indicates investors are
detaching from the volatile macro and geopolitical backdrop
Emotion dipped markedly in Q3 Month-by-month
July 2026: A fear-driven sell-off in

semiconductor stocks, particularly in
Korea, was balanced by optimism from
strong earnings reports from major tech
firms and a significant drop in oil prices.
August 2026: An easing in inflation
pressures lowered expectations of Fed

earnings season and signs of de-
escalation in the Middle East
September 2026: Rising energy prices
and renewed uncertainty in the Middle
East fuelled a sell-off in global sovereign

bonds. Concerns about debt sustainability
and hawkish central bank commentary
weighed on equity markets despite some
encouraging economic signals.

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 1
A reallocation of global sovereign reserve assets is accelerating, spurred by
geopolitical uncertainty and the fear of trade and financial sanctions. Notably,
central banks are repatriating their physical assets, such as the Dutch and French

central banks which have recently moved gold reserves out of New York.
Furthermore, a World Gold Council survey reveals that 45% of global central banks
plan to increase their gold reserves over the next 12 months.
What the market underestimates: The market views the recent surges in gold prices
as temporary, cyclical safe-haven flows. In reality, sovereign institutions are
structurally preparing for a more fragmented global trade system where tariff

policies and sanctions may make dollar-clearing assets more difficult.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Interest rates: At a time when the "world is awash in debt", long-term interest rates
of some countries may be forced structurally higher to attract alternative private
capital.
Inflation: Unexpected dollar depreciation may increase the cost of imported goods
Equity Prices: High-growth US equity valuations, which are highly sensitive to the

long-term risk-free rate, may face multiple compression if bond yields are pushed
higher.
The market currently prices in a material, long-term GDP boost from AI-driven
productivity gains. However, some research from the IMF suggests that over-reliance
on standardised LLMs can produce a high degree of "sameness" and uniformity in
cognitive output. Furthermore, escalating intellectual property and copyright
litigation threaten to bifurcate the quality of AI models based strictly on developers'

licensing budgets.
What the market underestimates: Some investors assume that the exponential
curve of AI hardware capabilities translates directly into exponential economic
output. But there is a risk that if AI tools merely standardise corporate processes
rather than innovate them, and legal barriers restrict access to premium data, the
productivity boom may be less aggressive than expected.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
GDP growth: Structural growth ceiling if AI fails to deliver the multi-percentage

point boost to global productivity required to offset aging demographics.
Equity prices: A downward revaluation of the technology and software sector if
massive AI infrastructure expenditures fail to generate revenue according to existing
expectations.
Geopolitical fragmentation of global reserve assets The AI “sameness“ trap and the productivity stagnation1 2
8

### Page 10

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Emerging risks underestimated by the market – part 2
Advanced economies are facing a dual demographic crisis: rapidly aging populations
and underfunded pension systems. This is forcing some older people to remain in or
re-enter the workforce out of financial necessity rather than choice.

What the market underestimates: Lower unemployment rates are celebrated as a
sign of economic strength. However, in structurally ageing societies like Japan and
parts of Europe, the growth of the labour force is increasingly driven by part-time,
older workers. Furthermore, G20 nations lack adequate, cohesive policy frameworks
to address these long-term demographic fiscal drains.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: An older workforce working out of necessity has less discretionary

spending power, potentially locking economies into persistent, structural demand-
side deflationary forces, offset by supply-side wage pressures in highly skilled
sectors.
GDP growth: Labour productivity growth that is lower than it might otherwise be as
the median age of the workforce climbs, limiting long-term potential GDP.
The geopolitical battleground is focussed on energy blockades (such as the Strait of
Hormuz) but underestimates the weaponisation of critical minerals. China currently

holds ‘escalation dominance’ over rare-earth mineral supply chains. The emerging
risk is the proactive restriction of these minerals to Western firms, such as recent
bans on supplying rare-earth inputs to some US firms.
What the market underestimates: The market treats rare-earth supply constraints as
temporary trade spat negotiation tactics. In reality, as Western nations aggressively
push for reshoring and friend-shoring, China is increasingly using its processing

monopoly as a long-term strategic blockade to try to slow Western high-tech,
defence and the energy transition.
Impact on key factors in our megatrend model:
Inflation: Upwards pressure on inflation as the cost of manufacturing electric
vehicles, semiconductors, and advanced electronics surges due to supply blockades
and the high capital cost of rebuilding localised processing facilities.
Interest rates: Central banks may be forced to keep policy rates higher to combat

the persistent supply-side inflation generated by supply-chain fragmentation.
The fragmentation of the EU’s political centre Critical mineral isolationism and ‘green’ supply chain
blockades3 4

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Emerging themes – part 1
Our dbLumina AI model helps us identify the key emerging themes that have the potential to
most impact markets and economies in the short term
While the previous focus was on the AI hardware squeeze and
commercial competition, the narrative is shifting towards AI's role as a

critical component of national security and geopolitical leverage.
— AI access: We see this in President Trump's framing of AI development as a
geopolitical race ("whoever wins AI wins").
— AI as a bargaining chip: Several examples over the last 12 months including the
2025 deal whereby AMD agreed to pay 15% of sales from certain AI chips to the
US government in exchange for an export license. Even if these deals change, the
trajectory is one where access to AI technology is tied to national economic and

security interests.
— Direct military & security applications: Several examples including, cyberattacks
on critical infrastructure (such as a UK power plant), the use of AI in developing
"exceptional" phages to fight E.coli, and the growing field of AI security
discussions.
Outlook: Expect more government intervention, the use of AI technology access as a
tool in diplomatic negotiations, and a greater focus on the dual-use nature of AI in
both commercial and military contexts. This will likely create regulatory and

operational uncertainty for tech firms.
The trend of reshoring is evolving into a more proactive diplomatic
strategy where nations are forming new, often transactional, alliances
to navigate a world less dominated by a single superpower.
— Proactive alliance building: Canadian Prime Minister Carney has been front and
centre making his first international trip to France and the UK in an effort to
diversify away from traditional reliance on the US. That follows other key

meetings between Japan and India, and more.
— Bypassing traditional powers: Greenland signalled its willingness to turn to
China for investment if Western countries do not provide support, reinforcing the
leverage of some smaller nations.
— Shifting security arrangements: The US pushed for a ‘reassurance force’ in
Ukraine, while French President Macron hosted a "coalition of the willing" to
discuss Europe's defence without US guarantees. Further examples include the

discussion of a Franco-German "nuclear sharing" arrangement.
Outlook: The assumptions of a bipolar world order is giving way to a more complex,
multi-nodal system. This will create greater emphasis on regional blocs and issue-
specific coalitions. The stability of traditional alliances like NATO and the G7 remain
deeply uncertain.
AI’s impact on geopolitics and national security The fracturing of multilateralism and the rise of "Middle
Power Countries" 1 2

Luke.Templeman@db.com
How Q3’s emerging themes shape the coming 12 months | October 2026 | Luke Templeman | Luke.Templeman@db.com Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Emerging themes – part 2
These four key themes dbLumina identifies are sometimes ‘known’ by investors, however, they may be underestimated by the market,
particularly when there are some potentially series longer-term impacts
Previous talk of “sovereign fiscal fragility” has now manifested itself

into a severe market reaction. Although fears around government debt
and deficits pop up from time to time, the magnitude of September’s
bond sell-off has been compounded by oil price concerns
— Accelerating sovereign sell-off: Concerns about inflation and debt sustainability
are not new but the peaks in bond yields are. Yields on 10-year Treasuries, bunds,
and gilts all rose sharply, with bunds hitting their highest level since 2009.
— Fiscal policy as a direct market catalyst: The market is increasingly sensitive to

fiscal policy announcements. For example, in the UK postponed tax rises, led to a
spike in gilt yields. Similarly, France's political turmoil and concerns over its
budget deficit caused the Franco-German yield spread to widen significantly.
— The “awash in debt" narrative: Treasury Secretary Bessent's comment at the G20
that "The world is awash in debt" signalled that the issue is increasingly top-of-
mind for policymakers.
Outlook: Markets will be increasingly quick to punish any perceived fiscal

irresponsibility. Expect higher borrowing costs, currency volatility, and increased risk
of financial instability, especially for countries with high debt levels.
The strain in the US consumer and labour markets is now diverging
sharply from the health of large corporations, reinforcing the K-shaped
economy.
— Strong corporate performance: Earnings season for Q2 showed remarkably solid
global growth, and the resilience of large corporations was a recurring theme

that helped support equity markets despite other headwinds.
— Weak consumer & labour signals: Economic reports have showed some signs of
a weakening labour market, falling consumer confidence, and stress among low-
income Americans. The New York Fed's survey also showed households expect a
worse financial situation.
— Policy and market disconnect: While strong corporate performance depends on
a robust economy, the underlying consumer weakness is concerning. Expect the

market to continue its twitchy reaction to data, as investors struggle to reconcile
these conflicting signals.
Outlook: This theme is potentially the most consequential for the domestic US
economy. If corporate profits and equity markets "detach" from the real economy as
consumers perceive it, it could lead to significant social and political instability,
making economic policy even more difficult to navigate.
The return of global bond vigilantes The bifurcation of economies: corporate resilience vs.

consumer strain3 4

GROWTH MOMENTUM TO SUSTAIN, 
EXTERNAL RISKS LINGER

India registered  strong growth at the start of 
FY27. Sustaining this performance will depend 
increasingly on how effectively it navigates a 
more uncertain global environment. Besides 
trade fragmentation, geopolitical tensions 
leading to higher energy prices, and shifts in 
international capital flows present key external 
risks.

India joined its Southeast Asian peers in 
posting an upside surprise on growth in 
1QFY27 (Apr -Jun26), up a firm 7.8%  yoy. few 
points of note:  
A) strong 1Q growth print reinforced the view 
that India’s domestic demand cycle was more 
resilient than initially assumed, with 
consumption, public capex and manufacturing 
providing a solid base despite external shocks.  
B) prevailing tailwinds by way of indirect tax 
relief in 4Q25, real rate buffer, and easier 
monetary conditions (earlier cuts), were also 
supportive;  
C) nominal GDP growth returned above 10% 
after two years on higher deflators, though 
some segments like manufacturing faced 
negative deflators as input prices rose at a 
faster pace than output due to commodity price 
increases and import intensity;  
D) Revisions to backdated numbers, to account 
for output PPI series, pushed up FY26 growth to 
7.8% (from 7.7% earlier). Two prior years were 
also revised.

www.dbs.com/insightsdirect/macro groupresc@dbs.com 
• High frequency indicators 
might keep  2QFY growth 
close to 7%. 
• Pace to moderate in 2HFY. 
• Retail inflation is likely to 
continue ris e and broaden  in 
scope, necessitating a tighter 
policy bias.  WPI likely to r ise 
by double-digits. 
• Special swap windows helped 
to stem bearish INR 
positioning. 
• Organic capital flows need to 
catch up.  
• Liquidity management is in 
focus, with a moderate 
correlation to inflation. 
• Twin-deficit risks are partly 
offset by stronger macro 
buffers.

High-frequency indicators

Into July -August, high frequency 
indicators are holding up, including GST 
e-way bills, indirect tax  revenues, 
electricity demand and growth in digital 
payments, while toll collections and 
petroleum consumption slipped . For 
demand, a contained extent of increase 
in retail pump prices shielded 
households from the surge in global 
crude prices, but other market linked 
fuel variants have climbed in recent 
months. On the employment front, job 
reductions were witnessed in the 
software sector, even as fresh 
opportunities were reported in global 
capability centres , electr onics/ 
semiconductors, healthcare, auto/ 
ancillary etc.  Wage growth in services 
has improved , manufacturing has been 
steady, while farm sector moderated, 
according t o consumption surveys by 
CMIE.

Industry indicators are a mixed bag, with 
PMIs maintaining their foothold in the 
expansionary terrain, while production 
and eight core index growth come off 
highs. Auto production continues to 
expand, though output and sales might 
moderate in the next quarter on base 
effects due to indirect tax cuts in the 
same time year ago. Forward-looking 
firm surveys have improved, after 
initially coming under pressure.

Sectors associated with rural demand  
might moderate amid weaker kharif 
sowing activity and lower reservoir 
levels impacting the rabi crop activity. 
Cumulative southwest monsoon is ~15% 
below the long-term average by tail end 
of the season. This takes the country on 
course to face its driest monsoon since 
2009 (which was 18% below LPA). 
Uneven geographical spread is notable, 
with south and east India registering 
nearly 25% shortfall.  While k harif 
sowing is on track with 99% of acreage 
completed, the winter crop might come 
under pressure as weather 
developments suggest El Niño  could 
extend into 2027.

### Page 3

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

With nominal GDP growth to track 11-12% 
this fiscal yea r, corporate earning s 
growth is expected to fare better than 
recent few years. 1QFY27 performance 
had already set off the year on a strong 
note, barring selected sectors like O&G 
which faced sequential marginal 
pressures from operating costs. Add to 
this, the centre’s capex gr owth is in firm 
double-digits, also backed by growth in 
core imports (non-oil non-gold).

On the external sector , despite better 
performance by exports, oil and 
electronics purchases have weighed on 
the goods math. The trade deficit is set 
to stay wide amidst elevated global 
crude cost, higher industrial fuels, 
cooking gas etc,  even as a wider mix of 
suppliers have addressed energy 
shortages. Notably, the net electronics 
deficit has widened considerably, just 
as exports have improved. Likewise, core 
imports (ex oil ex gold) have stayed firm, 
which partly feeds into higher exports as 
well as broader domestic demand. 
Monthly service trade surplus has been 
rangebound around $17 -18bn in recent 
months dwarfed by $25 -$30bn goods 
gap.

Maintaining growth forecasts

High frequency indicators put 2QFY 
growth close to 7%. We expect full year 
FY27 growth to average 7.3% yoy  from 
a revised 7.8% in FY26 , assuming 
moderation in the momentum in the 
second half of the year on tighter policy 
conditions, lagged impact of high energy 
prices and base effects from indirect tax 
cuts last year fade.  Assuming some 
spillover impact of exogenous 
uncertainties and tight financial 
conditions at home, we expect FY28 
growth to average 6.8-7.0%.

INFLATION OFF ITS BACK – 
THREE CATALYSTS

After two years of benign inflation, 
India’s price trend is off its back this 
year, mainly on the back of three

### Page 4

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

catalysts: Food, energy and early signs 
of demand pass through.

Aug inflation rose to a 20 -month high of 
4.8% yoy from 4.5% month before . As 
the chart highlights, number of sub -
segments growing by faster than the 
inflation target at 4% have continued to 
climb, pointing to a broadening out in the 
momentum.

Food inflation is gradually becoming 
more broad -based, shifting beyond 
vegetables and pulses to sugar, milk, 
protein groups, edible oils, amongst 
others. Sub-par weather has contributed 
to this upmove, besides idiosyncratic 
factors like swing in production  trends, 
diversion for fuel usage etc. After two 
years of normal monsoon, cumulative 
southwest rainfall this year is currently 
15% below the long -term average, 
alongside slower build -up in reservoir 
levels, which could impact upcoming rabi 
crops as well.  El-Nino patterns continue 
to strengthen, adding to seasonal swings 
and risk of impacting farm output.

Beyond food, g lobal oil prices have 
averaged ~$91pb in CY26, staying above 
$100pb for much of Seo26 . Retail fuel 
prices were adjusted in quick succession 
back in May26 and have been left 
unchanged since then , while CNG, 
aviation fuel, LPG etc have  continued to 
climb.

Transport inflation jumped to 4.6% yoy in 
Aug vs 0.1% in Jan26, besides utilities’ 
component inching up. Business price 
expectation surveys (conducted by IIM 
Ahmedabad) have, nonetheless, 
captured businesses facing a notable 
increase in fuel costs, especially 
industrial fuels, base metals and 
derivative product groups. Imported 
price pressures are most notabl e in the 
producer price/ wholesale price indices. 
These gauges have been hovering at 9 -
10% yoy in recent months.

### Page 5

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

Lastly, core inflation  (ex-food and fuel)  
rose to 1.5-month high in August, ma inly 
due to a sequential rebound in precious 
metals – gold and silver jewellery. 
Excluding precious metals, the reading 
was more moderate at 2.9%, pointing to 
some extent of economic slack. 
Nonetheless, other pockets like telecom, 
restaurants, business services, transport 
fares etc have been inching up, 
reflecting early signs of broadening in 
price pressures (on sequential basis), 
warranting attention. These are set 
against the backdrop of strong bank 
credit growth, flush INR liquidity and 
resilience in headline growth.

Our correlation analysis between 
liquidity (as % of NDTL) and core inflation 
– see chart, points to a positive but 
modest corelation at 0.4 between the 
two variables, with an assumed year -
worth lag on inflation. The inflation -
fighting mandate will also pus h the 
central bank to reduce the surplus 
liquidity in the domestic system.

A sh arp in global commodity prices is 
expected to fe ed into the wholesale 
price gauge , pushing up reading s to 
double-digits next quarter.

A gradual broadening of price 
pressures is likely to keep headline 
inflation above 5% in second half of the 
fiscal year , sharply narrowing the real 
rate buffer, underscoring the need for a 
tighter policy bias. Add to this, recent 
developments, including a sustained rise 
in crude prices, tightening global 
financial conditions, firm domestic 
growth and signs of broadening in core 
pressures, strengthen the case for a  
shallow 50bp hike in second half of FY27, 
making October’s meeting a live one.

TACKLING LIQUIDITY DELUGE, 
HIGHER YIELDS

Beating street estimates by a wide 
margin, the RBI’s special swap windows 
raised a cumulative $ 143bn into Sep , 
with the highest contribution (~92%) by

### Page 6

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

FCNR(B) deposits at $1 33bn, while rest 
were via offshore borrowing facilities. 
These flows pushed up the foreign 
reserves stock to a fresh high past 
$780bn, providing considerable 
firepower to defend the currency. This is 
already noticeable in the recent shift in 
the intervention bias. USDINR, which was 
earlier stubbornly steady in the face of 
strong FCNR inflows but  eased sharply 
due to strong intervention dollar sales 
and broader dollar swings, before being 
led higher due to external triggers.

Liquidity is a key watch factor. Given the 
swap arrangement, these inflows have 
added to already abundant INR liquidity 
backdrop, which was at a four year high, 
depressing overnight rates. The banking 
system surplus jumped past INR 10trn 
surpassing 2022 and Covid highs, with 
the durable balance widening sharply 
higher to INR 14trn, suppressing 
overnight call rates. This compares to 
average INR 1trn surplus in late-June.

While organic drivers like tax -related 
outflows, and seasonal currency leakage, 
in addition to CAD, portfolio outflows, 
and maturity of the forwards book will 
act as counter -balancing factors, yet 
concerted steps were required to drain 
the potential surge in liquidity.

We discussed the various seasonal, 
near-term and permanent measures  
that the central bank might consider (see 
FCNR bonanza necessitates deft 
management) to absorb this surfeit. 
Policymakers are being mindful of both 
the liquidity and signalling effects of 
the sterilisation tools at their disposal. 
The RBI utilised tranches of market 
operations like VRRR across tenors, 
after which three tranches of open 
market operations (bonds maturing FY29 
and FY32) worth a cumulative INR 1trn 
were conducted. These announcements 
fuelled a rise in yields, which were 
already inching up, tracking global 
counterparts. Sell/buy FX swaps for  
shorter rollovers were also being 
reportedly tapped.

When the dust settles, focus will also 
be on the bunched -up maturities that 
will fall due in 3Y and 5Y tenor of the 
deposits. A portion of the existing 
reserve stock could be earmarked 
against these liabilities, helping to 
mitigate concerns that deposit 
maturities or debt repayments could 
trigger a sharp increase in dollar demand 
and exert pressure on the FX market 
down the line. In the near-term, priorities 
will be to manage liquidity, gradually 
lower the sizeable forwards book, and 
support the domestic currency. Long -
end yields continue to be influenced by 
the hardening global yields.

BALANCE OF PAYMENTS MATH 
TO BE COMFORTABLE

The FY27 current account math is 
expected to display two key counter 
trends, Firstly, the wider goods trade 
imbalance will raise the FY27 current 
account deficit. Secondly, the 
counterbalancing effect of a sharp 
improvement in the capital flows math 
will keep the annual balance of 
payments math in surplus.

### Page 7

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            7

India recorded a current account deficit 
(CAD) of $10.5bn during April-July 2026, 
compared with $6.1bn in the 
corresponding period last year. The 
deterioration was driven almost entirely 
by a wider merchandise trade deficit, 
which rose to $117.8bn from $97.1bn a 
year ago. Part of this offset by higher  
income, transfers and capital account 
flow. Capital flows found support from 
net FDI, portfolio investments and 
banking capital, especially NRI deposits 
(jumped sharply to $36bn vs $4.6bn 
same period last year) , pushing up Apr -
Jul26 BOP to $12.7bn surplus, more than 
double of $4.8bn same time last year.

Oil prices are the main swing factor for 
the CAD this year , with the current run -
rate tracking our forecast at -1.1% of GDP 
for FY27. This suggests that the 
widening in the CAD is commodity -price 
related rather than driven by higher 
demand.

On the other hand, capital flows are 
being buttressed by the special swap 
windows introduced by the central bank, 
which raised a cumulative $ 143bn 
between June and August, through non -
resident deposits and external 
borrowings. This has helped to fil l the 
gap from subdued portfolio flows and 
net investment trends. We expect FY27 
current account deficit to track -1.1% of 
GDP in FY27, with the balance of 
payments to register upwards of $80bn 
surplus (vs -$23.6bn deficit last year).  
Into FY28, the dependency of capital 
flows on o rganic drivers like portfolio 
flows and FDI is set to increase, 
underscoring the need for higher 
vigilance.

FISCAL RUN-RATE

The FY27 fiscal run -rate points to a 
stronger revenue momentum, which 
has helped to keep the FYTD deficit in 
check, even as expenditure was 
frontloaded owing to subsidy payments 
and capex disbursements. April-July 
2026 deficit stands at a third of the full-
year balance.

### Page 8

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            8

Both tax and non -tax receipts fared 
better than the comparable period year 
ago, helped by stronger corporate tax 
collections, STT transaction revenues, 
and RBI dividend, amongst others.

Setting 2QFY for a strong start, net 
direct tax collections r ose 13% yoy by 
mid-Sep26, driven by net corporate tax 
collections up 19.5%. Net non -corporate 
tax collections were up 6%, besides STT 
collections up a sharp 53% yoy. Add to 
this, divestments have also fared better 
than expected this year, with ~78% 
raised by August, accompanied by 
dividends & profits (at 83% of budget by 
Jul26).

Under expenditure, about 33% of the full 
year allocation was allocated by July 
2026. The subsidy outgo rose sharply, up 
35% yoy, led by higher food at 23% and 
fertiliser at 46% (already accounting for 
nearly two-third of the full year budget)  
led by surging global DAP and urea 
prices. With global prices remaining high, 
the actual outgo might be higher than 
budgeted INR 1.7trn , likely towards INR 
2.5trn. Capital spending also remained 
apace, up 30% yoy in first five months of 
the year.

Overall, strong growth in tax and non-tax 
receipts allowed the centre to sustain 
higher spending while containing the 
extent of fiscal slippage until July 2026 
(FYTD). A strong nominal GDP rate 
(helped by higher inflation) will also 
provide a favourable denominator effect.

IN SUM

The economy exhibited resilience in the 
face of a tough external environment in 
the first quarter, with part of that 
stability likely to extend to 2QFY27 as 
well. Tighter monetary conditions, El 
Nino impact and base effects from 
passage of post -indirect tax jump will 
moderate headline growth in 2HFY. 
Nonetheless for the year, we expected 
growth to stay north of 7%, factoring in a 
strong start.

### Page 9

India: Growth, liquidity and policy response September 22, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            9

► 聯準會9月升息1碼至3.75%-4.00%，最新點
陣圖顯示2026年仍有再升息一碼空間 ，2027
年傾向維持利率不變 ，2028年後才逐步回到
降息趨勢 。主席華許重申抑制通膨目標 ，升
息立場官員數明顯較前次會議增加 ，凸顯聯
準會鷹派政策態度 ，惟利率路徑較市場原先
預期的連續緊縮和緩，市場偏利多解讀。
► 聯準會 本次亦上修 2026-2027年GDP成長預
估，下修失業率預測 ，反映美國 經濟與勞動
市場尚未明顯轉弱；另一方面 ，能源價格上
升與成本轉嫁使整體及核心通膨預估再度上

修；景氣具韌性 、通膨回落速度偏慢 ，使聯
準會仍有條件維持較緊縮的政策立場 。在高
利率環 境可 能延 續 下，股 票評價 仍面 臨壓
力，配置上可相對偏向獲利與現金流穩定 、
防禦性較高的產業；債券則以中短天期 、高
信評企業債為主要方向 ，把握目前較高的收
益率。長天期公債仍受通膨 、財政紀律及期
限溢酬上升影響 ，宜待通膨與景氣出現更明
確降溫訊號後，再提高配置。

► 日本8月CPI年增1.9%，油價上漲帶來的外溢
影響，逐漸反應在消費成本上。7月勞工平均
薪資較去年同期成長 4.7%，創1997年1月以
來最大增幅；薪資增速追上通膨 ，帶動實質
薪資增長 。此外 ，第二季 GDP季增年率達
1.4%優於預期，8月製造業PMI因AI及半導體
需求暢旺上升，7月出口年增率高達23.2%，
創2022年10月來最快增速 。整體通膨升溫加
上景氣復甦，為日銀升息提供底氣。
► 日本央行會後 聲明稿表示：金融環境實質利
率偏低 ，將視情況調整貨幣政策 ，並強調升
息後金融環境仍維持寬鬆 ，未如市場預期的
鷹派，使日股持續上漲 、日圓回貶 。考量日
銀呵護 寬鬆金融環境 ，侷限日圓續強力 道，
但在美 國施 壓與 日 銀未來 仍可能 升息 背景
下，日圓難回到過去極弱 狀態。相較過去 ，
現在升值的日圓對日本出口 、科技股壓力可
控，日本10年公債殖利率來到3%，利率上升
有利 金融 股 ，投 資 短期或 以金融 、內 需為

主，科技、半導體股逢回可分批布局。

投資焦點低波價值防禦
► 美、歐、日主要央行連袂升息 ，加上美伊衝
突惡化 ，國際油價高漲 ，通膨疑慮與成熟國
家債務壓力 ，使全球多數國家公債殖利率衝
高，市場擔憂高利率與高油價可能拖累經濟
成長，全球股債匯市承壓 、波動上升 ，並以
此前領漲多頭行情的晶片 、半導體和科技股
最明顯 。觀察今年以來 ，不止股市波動度上
升，債市的波動更不亞於 股票市場 ，統計過
去，當VIX恐慌指數與MOVE債券波動度走高
時，低波動或防禦股的表現 ，往往比成長股
更好，顯示當市場震盪加劇時 ，資金傾向轉
往穩健資產停泊。
► 最近一次聯準會升息 ，為2022 年因疫情爆
發、供應鏈中斷 ，導致通膨大幅上揚 ，迫使
央行採取緊縮貨幣政策 ，和目前通膨上升促
央行升息背景 類似。前次聯準會啟動升息後
數月，成長股 、費城半導體股表現受抑 ，防
禦股、價值股和低波動族群 ，在剛開始升息
的環境下 ，表現相對抗跌 。考量目前聯準 會
籠罩在升息陰霾下，可增加相關類股配置。
對等關稅宣布後，內需防禦股獲利下修幅度最少 最近一次聯準會升息後，防禦低波高息和價值股突出美國股債波動度上升時，低波動、防禦股表現較佳

► 觀察最近一季 ，資訊科技 、通訊服務與非核
心消費等High Beta類股，表現明顯落後於大
盤；反觀價值股 、防禦股和低波動等因子 ，
甚至核 心消 費與 高 股利等 傳統類 防禦 性產
業，報酬相對較 穩健，受資金青睞 。顯示在
經歷過 上半 年科 技 、半導 體股的 大漲 行情
後，市場預期高 利率與地緣政治 風險可能維
持更久時間 ，重新聚焦在獲利持久、現金易
落袋為安的防禦型資產，更能持盈保泰。
► 從預估本 益比看，目前金融僅 16倍、防禦股
17.5倍、價值股 (以IWD ETF代表)17.9倍、
低波動 (以USMV ETF代表)19.6倍，皆低於
標普500指數 現在 的21.3倍，近月資金流向
物美價廉的低波動價值 、防禦族群，評價越
低、漲幅越高 。展望後市，AI長線基本面仍
持續看好 ，股市回檔或為評價 修正而 非盈餘
下滑，市場波動度提升之際 ，建議不過度 集
中於單一 產業或 個股，多元配置適度增加 低
波動 、 防禦 和價 值股，分 批布局 以控管 風

險，料將更能在震盪加劇時安穩求利。
投資焦點
近季資金青睞波動度較低的防禦、價值和低波動類股
資金輪動，關注波動度低和評價合理的防禦價值族群

股市
 美、歐、日央行升息抑通膨 ，利率創高添股市波動風險 ，加上成熟國家債務攀升 ，科技巨擘亦舉債
投入資本支出 ，未來獲利與變現 能力持續被市場檢視 ，金融市場震盪不減 。美伊衝突升溫，近期可
透過防禦、低波動或價值 股來降低風險；科技 及AI半導體等發展趨勢不變 ，獲利基本面佳 ，長線發
展可期，宜採逢回分批布局方式，分散國家與產業配置。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，全球利率彈升有利銀行股 ，歐、日銀行股基本面佳 ，歐洲國防軍工
仍具投資吸引力。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融等產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升，應避免超長天期債券投資 ，預期年底前美國 10年期公債利率將於 4.5%-5.0%
震盪，可逢利率走升時，以中短天期債券進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款
能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。

債種：中天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金融、
科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息後，年底前或明年初仍可能再度緊縮，日圓震盪加大偏升。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪加大偏升
商品
 沙烏地阿拉伯關閉輸油管線 ，加上荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張 ，能源供需失衡 ，油價年底前維持 偏高水位。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力 與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法

### Page 16

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

THIS WEEK IN EARNINGS 
AGGREGATE ESTIMATES AND REVISIONS 
 26Q3 Y/Y earnings are expected to be 30.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the Y/Y earnings estimate is 26.8%. 
 Of the 13 companies in the S&P 500 that have reported earnings to date for 26Q3, 84.6% have reported earnings 
above analyst estimates. This compares to a long-term average of 67.7% and prior four quarter average of 81.5%. 
 26Q3 Y/Y revenue is expected to be 11.6%. Excluding the energy sector, the growth estimate is 11.2%. 
 76.9% of companies have reported 26Q3 revenue above analyst expectations. This compares to a long-term 
average of 63% and an average over the past four quarters of 76.8%. 
 For 26Q3, there have been 40 negative EPS preannouncements issued by S&P 500 corporations compared to 65 
positive EPS preannouncements. By dividing 40 by 65 the N/P ratio is 0.6 for the S&P 500 Index.  
 The forward four-quarter (26Q4– 27Q3) P/E ratio for the S&P 500 is 20.1. 
 During the week of Oct. 5, three S&P 500 companies are expected to report quarterly earnings.

There has been an increase in the share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 since the start of the quarter (to $773.2B 
from $760.7B). Seven of the eleven sectors have experienced downward revisions to estimates.

Since Aug. 1, the materials (-9.1%) and health care (-2.2%) sectors have recorded the highest percentage decreases 
in earnings, while the energy (16.3%) sector has recorded the highest percentage increase in earnings. Overall, share-
weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 1.6% since the start of the quarter.

Since Aug. 1, the health care (-1.7B) and materials (-1.5B) sectors have recorded the highest dollar-level decreases in 
earnings, while the information technology (8.3B) sector has recorded the highest dollar-level increase in earnings. 
Overall, expected share-weighted earnings for the S&P 500 have increased by 12.5B since the start of the quarter.

In aggregate, companies are reporting earnings that are 5.3% above estimates, which compares to a long-term (since

The estimated earnings growth rate for the S&P 500 for 26Q3 is 30.6%. If the energy sector is excluded, the growth 
rate declines to 26.8%. The S&P 500 expects to see share-weighted earnings of $773.2B in 26Q3, compared to share-
weighted earnings of $591.9B (based on the year-ago earnings of the current 504 constituents) in 25Q3. 
11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q3. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Consumer Staples 
sector has the weakest anticipated growth compared to 25Q3. 
The Energy sector has the highest earnings growth rate (114.7%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $55.1B in 26Q3, 
compared to earnings of $25.7B in 25Q3. 4 of the 5 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see higher earnings 
than a year ago. The Oil & Gas Refining & Marketing (445.6%) and Integrated Oil & Gas (116.1%) sub-industries have 
the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate declines to 25.1%. 
The Information Technology sector has the second highest earnings growth rate (66.5%) of any sector. It is expected 
to earn $256.1B in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $153.8B in 25Q3. 12 of the 12 sub-industries in the sector are 
anticipated to see higher earnings than a year ago. The Semiconductors (135.6%) and Semiconductor Materials & 
Equipment (68.9%) sub-industries have the highest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, 
the growth rate declines to 25.3%. 
The Consumer Staples sector has the lowest earnings growth rate (3.2%) of any sector. It is expected to earn $33.7B 
in 26Q3, compared to earnings of $32.6B in 25Q3. 3 of the 10 sub-industries in the sector are anticipated to see lower 
earnings than a year ago. The Packaged Foods & Meats (-6.5%) and Household Products (-4.2%) sub-industries have 
the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 6.4%.

11 of the 11 sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in earnings relative to 25Q4. The Energy and 
Information Technology sectors have the highest earnings growth rates for the quarter, while the Real Estate sector

earnings than a year ago. The Telecom Tower Reits (-13.3%) and Multi-Family Residential Reits (-0.1%) sub-industries 
have the lowest earnings growth in the sector. If these sub-industries are removed, the growth rate improves to 7.1%.

STARMINE EARNINGS SURPRISE FORECAST
Looking forward at quarterly performance, we use StarMine’s SmartEstimate® from LSEG to determine which
companies in the S&P 500 are better poised to beat and miss earnings estimates. The SmartEstimate® is a weighted

average of analyst estimates, with more weight given to more recent estimates and more accurate analysts. Our
studies have shown that when the SmartEstimate® differs significantly from the consensus (I/B/E/S mean estimate),
the Predicted Surprise accurately predicts the direction of earnings surprises or further revisions 70% of the time.
When a significant Predicted Surprise for revenue is also present for the period, the accuracy improves to 78%.

StarMine ARM from LSEG is an analyst revisions stock ranking model, designed to predict future changes in analyst
sentiment. ARM incorporates more accurate earnings estimates through the SmartEstimate prediction service. ARM
region rankings scores companies by region on a scale of 1 to 100 where 100 represents the most bullish sentiment.
Over the next two weeks, 31 S&P 500 companies are expected to report earnings. Of these companies, three positive

surprises and five negative surprises are expected from S&P 500 companies reporting quarterly results.

Document 286: Q3 Job Vacancies_ another step lower (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Job Vacancies_ another step lower (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #labour #employment
Q3 Job Vacancies: another step lower
By Neha Sharma
Economist, Westpac
13:07 October 01 2026 Share
The number of job vacancies fell by 3.0k (–0.9%qtr) between May and August. The decline
was wholly driven by the private sector, where vacancies fell 2.0%qtr. In contrast, public
sector vacancies rose 7.8%qtr.

### Page 2

Job vacancies fell by 3.0k (–0.9%qtr) between May and August, extending the previous quarter’s
decline. The May and February estimates were also revised slightly lower. As a result, vacancies are
now 1.4% below their level a year earlier. The result was at odds with partial indicators, which all
pointed to a rise in the quarter.

The decline was wholly driven by the private sector, where vacancies fell 2.0%qtr to be 1.5%yr lower.
In contrast, public sector vacancies rose 7.8%qtr, marking the strongest quarterly increase in four years
and partially reversing the sharp 7.5%qtr decline recorded in the May quarter. Public sector vacancies
were flat over the year.

Job vacancies have largely moved sideways since early 2025, though a resumption of the broader
downtrend now appears increasingly likely. Labour demand has held up reasonably well, though not
sufficiently to match growth in labour supply, resulting in a gradual easing in labour market conditions
(see here). Today’s result is also consistent with our view that this process will continue into early 2027
as the cumulative effects of high inflation and higher interest rates continue to weigh on activity and
hiring demand.

### Page 3

The vacancy-to-unemployment ratio, a key measure of labour market tightness closely monitored by
the RBA, declined from 0.48 to 0.45. The fall was the sharpest since early 2025, reflecting both a
decline in vacancies and a 6.8%qtr increase in the number of unemployed people over the quarter,
largely owing to the continued strength in labour supply. The ratio remains well above its long-run
average of around 0.2.

Given the strong growth in labour supply, the Beveridge curve provides a useful framework for
assessing recent labour market developments. By comparing vacancies and unemployment as shares
of the labour force, it controls for growth in the overall size of the labour market. Between 2022 and
2024, the labour market moved down the steep portion of the Beveridge curve, with substantial
declines in vacancies occurring alongside only modest increases in unemployment. While labour
market tightness has continued to unwind, Australia remains in this region of the curve, suggesting that
further rebalancing between labour demand and supply can occur through lower vacancies without a
material rise in unemployment.

### Page 4

All statistics mentioned below are not seasonally adjusted and hence may not add up to the seasonally
adjusted statistics referenced above.

By industry, job vacancies declined in eight of the 18 industries over the three months to August. The
largest falls were recorded in wholesale trade (–18.2%qtr), professional, scientific & technical services
(–17.4%qtr), and information media & telecommunications (–16.7%qtr). Consumer-facing sectors were
mixed, with retail trade (24.6%) and arts & recreation services (10.3%) recording strong increases,
while accommodation & food services edged 0.9% lower. Only three industries have seen vacancies
fall below their pre-pandemic levels: administrative & support services (–24.3%qtr), information media
& telecommunications (–9.1%qtr), and financial & insurance services (–7.3%qtr).

Overall market sector vacancies were flat in the quarter, meanwhile the non-market sector saw a 2.9%
drop, owing to declines in education & training (–8.2%qtr) and health care & social assistance (–
4.2%qtr). For detailed insights on industry condition, see Q2 Labour Account and the Westpac
Business Signal.

Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• Consumer spending is holding up, and we expect real PCE to rise at around a 3.0% annualized rate
in Q3.
Consumer spending has been mixed, yet resilient in recent months. After a soft July, driven largely by

weaker durable goods purchases, retail sales rebounded strongly in August, rising at a 1.2% monthly
clip amid broadly distributed gains across retailers. The latest data have led us to lift our near-term
growth forecast, and we now look for real PCE to rise at a 3.2% annualized clip in Q3. Previous data
will also likely be revised higher on firmer control group sales data and strong services revenue in Q2,
suggesting recent consumer momentum remains intact. All told, the consumer is still in decent shape

and has capacity to keep spending. Real income growth remains modest but is beginning to improve
as inflation eases and the labor market remains broadly supportive suggesting continued consumption
even if that pace of spending may moderate into year-end.
Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build-out is still king in terms of the contribution to business investment, but the
broadening in capex spending beyond the tech sector is taking root.

AI-related investment continues to dominate capital allocation. There are no signs of firms scaling
back their outlays now, though at some point as AI investment matures, merely sustaining spending
at elevated levels will provide a smaller incremental boost to growth than it has in recent quarters.
Beyond AI, the tentative signs we've identified that signal a broadening in the investment backdrop
are getting a bit louder. The ISM manufacturing index has been in expansion territory for eight-

Economics | 3

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
straight months, and durable goods orders have firmed across a wider range of industries. Part of
this improvement likely reflects delayed capital spending plans finally moving forward, but inventory
rebuilding after five consecutive quarters of drawdown may be lending some support as well.

Source: U.S. Department of Commerce and Wells Fargo Economics
Investment: Residential
• Residential construction looks likely to taper through the rest of the year while mortgage rates and
new home inventories remain elevated.
Headwinds have intensified against residential construction. The trend in single-family construction is
downshifting, emblematic of the affordability challenges depressing buyer demand. As of this writing,

mortgage rates have risen to over 7.0% and builder confidence has declined alongside. Sales stuck in
a low gear mean that new home listings are inching higher, further discouraging new development.
Multifamily construction appears to be holding up but faces limited potential upside with vacancy
rates elevated across much of the South. We still see a potential for marginal improvement in 2027,
but higher mortgage rates dampen our outlook.
Investment: Nonresidential Structures

• Construction appears to be gaining steam, led by data centers and power. However, sluggish
commercial starts elsewhere and high interest rates are likely to keep growth relatively modest.
The AI infrastructure build out is supporting modest growth in nonresidential construction. After
moderating for a full year, private nonresidential outlays rose from April to July. Data centers and
power are propelling current momentum. Though construction has also strengthened across sports

facilities and communication infrastructure, commercial projects remain stuck in low gear. Categories
like warehousing and retail remain soft, and overall architectural billings have improved only modestly.
Looking ahead, a recent breakout in megaprojects is likely to support additional data center and
manufacturing development, while higher interest rates limit overall growth.

Net Exports & Trade
• Net exports are tracking to slice nearly two-percentage points off headline GDP growth amid
strong imports.
Imports are shaping up for another strong quarter after a surge in July and a rebound in August
retail spending suggesting continued domestic demand for product. The July import gain was again

almost entirely driven by high-tech related goods like computers & accessories, semiconductors and
telecommunications equipment. We still anticipate a bit more of a broadening out in imports to come,
perhaps as early as August as businesses replenish inventory amid continued consumer demand.
Export growth is a bit more constrained, suggesting an expansion in the trade deficit and that net
exports will slice nearly two-percentage points off headline GDP growth in Q3.

Labor Market
• We modestly raised our 2026 payroll forecast and made no material changes to our 2027 forecast.
We continue to expect the unemployment rate to end this year at 4.2%.
August's employment report reinforced that the labor market remains stable. Payroll growth has
averaged 106K over the past six months, though we expect some moderation in the months ahead.
Hiring measures from regional Fed surveys and hiring plans from small businesses have cooled from

their early summer jumps and job openings have leveled off. At the same time, initial jobless claims
remain near multi-decade lows and continue to indicate that layoffs remain contained. We expect
payroll growth to moderate to roughly 65K per month over the remainder of the year, a pace broadly
consistent with our estimate of breakeven. As hiring cools slightly and the participation rate rebounds
somewhat further, we see some scope for the unemployment rate to edge up to 4.2% by year-end.

Even with the slight increase though, labor market conditions would remain consistent with full
employment.
Inflation
• Incorporating our expectations for the upcoming BEA methodology changes and recent inflation
data, we look for the core PCE deflator to end the year at 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis, up from
3.1% in our previous forecast.
Headline inflation remains tied to the conflict in the Middle East, although underlying price pressures
continue to move in a more favorable direction. Higher oil prices are keeping some upward pressure

on energy-sensitive categories in the near term, but tariff-related goods inflation is fading and price
pressures outside a handful of technology-related categories remain limited. Meanwhile, core services
inflation remains on a moderating trend as shelter inflation continues to gradually cool and labor cost
pressures slowly ease. Taken together, inflation pressures appear less widespread than earlier this year,
Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
but recurring supply shocks are likely to keep the path back to 2% bumpy. We expect the core PCE
deflator to slow to 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis this year.

Fiscal Policy
• We forecast this year's federal budget deficit will total $2.1 trillion (~6.5% of GDP), and next year's
will be about the same in dollar terms and a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).

The federal budget deficit continues to hover in the 6.0-6.5% of GDP range, similar to where it was
at the end of the Biden administration. The outlook for the next couple of years is one of status quo.
We doubt there will be any new, big fiscal legislation with divided government likely after the midterm
elections, but rising interest costs and steady growth in mandatory spending programs such as Social
Security and Medicare will keep deficits from shrinking much. It's not just U.S. fiscal problems putting

upward pressure on long-term rates. Other factors such as robust corporate debt issuance, policy
uncertainty and foreign factors are also playing a role. Our base case for 6.0%-6.5% of GDP budget
deficits will not help when it comes to bringing down long-term interest rates.
6 | Economics

### Page 7

U.S. Economic Outlook: September 2026 Economics
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• We have changed our fed funds forecast to 4.00-4.25% by year-end 2026, with no changes in
2027. Our year-end forecasts for the 10-year Treasury yield are also higher at 4.90% and 4.75%,
respectively, for this year and next.
The FOMC made clear at its September 16 meeting that stubbornly high PCE inflation this year was
the key factor behind the Committee's decision to raise rates. Given this, we think only a string of

good inflation data can bail the Fed out, and accordingly we think one more rate hike is coming this
year, with softer inflation data in 2027 giving way to a more patient FOMC. We could easily envision
one more hike beyond that or a "one and done" hike where the FOMC does not go again. Outcomes
outside that range, however, strike us as unlikely. A rising neutral rate makes meaningful rate cuts hard
to justify in the absence of an economic slowdown, while the case for a more aggressive tightening

cycle seems weak given that the labor market is not overheating and much of the excess inflation is
supply side driven. We think a very short gradual tightening cycle and an eventual reduction in energy
prices/slowdown in inflation will allow Treasury yields to drift modestly lower in 2027. But, the factors
mentioned above (e.g., large budget deficits, rising neutral rate) should prevent yields from falling in a
bigger way.

International Developments
• Global growth has proved more resilient than expected, prompting us to upgrade our 2026 global
GDP growth forecast to 2.9%. We have also modestly revised our global inflation outlook higher,
lifting our 2026 CPI forecast to 4.4% and our 2027 forecast accordingly.
• We now expect more tightening across advanced economies than in our previous monthly forecast
update, while emerging market policy paths remain mixed.

Global economic activity proved more resilient than expected in H1-2026, with early indicators
suggesting that momentum carried into Q3. While precautionary stockpiling supported some of the
earlier strength, activity has remained resilient on firmer services, continued AI-related investment and
exports (U.S., South Korea, Japan and Australia) and increased defense spending (Eurozone and Japan).
We expect inflation to remain higher for longer before easing next year and averaging 4% in 2027 as

tighter financial conditions weigh on demand and supply pressures fade. Risks remain skewed to the
upside. Oil and product inventories remain below pre-conflict levels, while limited refining capacity and
ongoing disruptions leave energy prices vulnerable to renewed pressure. El Niño and higher fertilizer
costs threaten food prices, while AI-related demand is raising semiconductor costs. Second-round
effects remain limited, but successive supply shocks increase the risk of persistent inflation into next

year.

Document 342: 「脱スターマー」を鮮明にした英バーナム政権 ～改革の方向性を示すも、実行を阻む厳しい財政制約～

Source PDF: all report pdf/「脱スターマー」を鮮明にした英バーナム政権 ～改革の方向性を示すも、実行を阻む厳しい財政制約～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Europe Trends

2026 年 10 月１日

1/4 
「脱スターマー」を鮮明にした英バーナム政権 
～改革の方向性を示すも、実行を阻む厳しい財政制約～ 
 経済調査部 首席エコノミスト  田中 理 
（℡：050-5474-7494）

（要旨） 
○ 英国で９月30 日、 バーナム首相の就任後で初となる労働党大会が閉幕した。 バーナム首相は
党首演説で、過去 40 年間に英国で進められてきた政策からの転換を訴え、社会的ケア、住
宅、エネルギー、水道など国民生活に密接に関わる分野で政府の関与を強める方針を表明し
た。年金制度や選挙制度の改革に加え、欧州連合（ＥＵ）との関係についても、スターマー
前政権が設定したレッドライン（政策上の譲れない一線）にとらわれず、中長期的な関係を
再検討する考えを示した。 
○ 一連の政策は、財政規律を重視し、市場や企業との関係に配慮して政策運営を行ったスター
マー前政権からの転換を印象づけるものとなった。ただ、バーナム政権が直面する財政環境
は厳しく、今回打ち出した改革の多くは直ちに実施される訳ではない。今回の党大会は、バ
ーナム政権が目指す英国の将来像を明確にする一方で、それを実現する財源や当面の経済政
策については多くの課題を残すものとなった。

■公共サービスへの政府関与を強化 
９月 27～30 日にリバプールで開催された英国の労働党大会では、７月に発足したバーナム政権
が今後の政策運営の方向性を示した。今回の党大会で鮮明になったのが、公共サービスへの政府
関与を強める方向性である。バーナム首相は、住宅、水道、エネルギーなど生活に不可欠な分野
について、民間企業や市場に過度に依存してきた従来の政策を見直す考えを示した。水道事業で
は政府による出資に対する制約を撤廃し、経営難に陥った事業者などへの公的関与を可能にす
る。住宅についても、自治体が空き家を取得し、低家賃住宅として活用するなど、住宅供給への
公的関与を強める。 
バーナム政権の政策転換を象徴するのが、全国共通の社会的ケア制度「国民ケアサービス
（National Care Service：ＮＣＳ） 」の創設である。次期議会中に高齢者や障害者に対する介
護・生活支援サービスの無償化を目指す。これは原則無料の医療サービスを提供する「国民保健
制度（National Health Service：ＮＨＳ） 」に似た制度で、自宅での訪問介護、食事・着替え・
入浴・トイレなどの日常生活支援、ケアホーム・介護施設での介護、障害者の自立生活支援など

### Page 2

2/4 
を、資産や所得の多寡にかかわらず利用できる。 
 ただ、社会的ケアの拡充には巨額の財源が必要となる。このためバーナム首相は、2030 年以降
に公的年金の「トリプルロック」を見直し、年金支給額の伸びを抑制する方針を表明した。トリ
プルロックとは、公的年金の受給額を毎年、 「賃金上昇率」 、 「物価上昇率」 、 「2.5％」のうち最も
高い率に合わせて引き上げる仕組みである。高齢者の所得が物価上昇や現役世代の賃金上昇から
取り残されることを防ぐことを目的に、2011 年に導入された。物価や賃金の伸びが低い場合でも
最低 2.5％の増額を保証するため、高齢者の所得保障を手厚くする一方で、年金支出が経済成長や
税収を上回って増加しやすく、財政負担の拡大につながるとの指摘もある。 
これは単なる社会保障支出の拡大とは性格が異なる。高齢者向けの現金給付である年金の伸び
を抑える一方、その財源を社会的ケアという現物サービスの拡充に振り向けるもので、高齢者向
け社会保障の中身を組み替える改革と位置づけられる。

■ブレグジットのレッドラインも見直しへ 
今回の党大会では、英国とＥＵとの関係についても大きな政策転換の方向性が示された。バー
ナム首相は、英国のＥＵ離脱（ブレグジット）が「英国に利益よりも害をもたらした」との認識
を示し、ＥＵとの長期的な関係を改めて検討する考えを表明した。さらに、党大会閉幕日の30 日
には、今後の選択肢として、現在の関係を維持するだけでなく、ＥＵの関税同盟への参加、単一
市場への参加、さらにはＥＵへの再加盟まで排除せず検討する考えを示した。 
 スターマー前政権は、ＥＵとの関係改善を進めた一方で、 「ＥＵには再加盟しない」 、 「単一市場
には復帰しない」 、 「関税同盟には復帰しない」というレッドライン（政策上の譲れない一線）を
維持してきた。バーナム首相の発言は、英国とＥＵの中長期的な関係について、こうした制約を
いったん外し、幅広い選択肢を比較する姿勢に転じたことを意味する。 
もっとも、現時点でバーナム政権がＥＵ再加盟を政策目標として決定した訳ではない。関税同
盟や単一市場への参加を含む複数の選択肢を検討し、今後の国民的な議論につなげるという段階
にある。それでも、ブレグジット後の英国では主要政党がＥＵ再加盟を現実的な政策選択肢とし
て扱うことは避けてきただけに、首相が再加盟まで排除しない姿勢を示した意味は小さくない。
11 月に予定される英ＥＵ首脳会議と、その前後に示される英国政府の長期的な対ＥＵ方針が次の
焦点となる。

■政治制度にも改革の矛先 
 バーナム政権は、公共サービスや対ＥＵ政策に加え、英国の選挙制度の見直しにも踏み込む。
バーナム首相は従来から、現在の小選挙区制（First Past the Post：FPTP）を見直し、得票率を
議席数により反映しやすい比例代表制を導入する必要性を訴えてきた。今回の党大会では、選挙
制度改革を検討する超党派の委員会（National Commission on Electoral Reform）を 2026 年中
に設置する方針を表明した。 
バーナム政権は現在の議会で選挙制度を変更する訳ではない。バーナム首相は９月１日の議会

### Page 3

3/4 
答弁で、次期総選挙前に制度を変更する考えを明確に否定した。これは、選挙制度のような政治
の基本ルールを変更するには、選挙を通じて国民から政策実行の正統性や権限を与えられる必要
があるとの考えによるものだ。このため、次期総選挙までは現行の小選挙区制を維持する。超党
派委員会がそれまでに選挙制度改革についての報告書をまとめ、その内容を踏まえて労働党が次
期総選挙のマニフェストに制度改革を盛り込む。選挙で国民の信任を得て次期政権を担えば、選
挙後の議会で制度変更を実施するという工程を想定している。 
導入する制度の具体的な形はまだ決まっていない。バーナム首相自身は比例代表制への移行を
支持しているが、比例代表制にも様々な方式があり、具体的にどの制度を採用するかについては
超党派委員会で検討することになる。このため、現段階では、 「比例性を高める方向で選挙制度を
見直す」という改革の方向性が示された段階と位置づけるのが適切だ。 
 英国の下院選挙では、各選挙区で最多得票を得た候補者だけが当選する小選挙区制が採用され
ている。この制度は単独政権を生みやすい一方、政党の全国得票率と議席数の間に大きな乖離が
生じる。2024 年の総選挙では、労働党が33.7％の得票で下院の650 議席のうち411 議席を獲得し
たのに対し、リフォームＵＫは14.3％の得票で５議席の獲得にとどまった。 
 比例性の高い制度に移行すれば、こうした得票率と議席数の乖離は縮小する一方で、一つの政
党が単独で下院の過半数を確保することが難しくなり、連立政権や少数与党政権が増える可能性
がある。また、リフォームＵＫのように全国的に一定の支持を集めながら小選挙区制では議席に
つながりにくかった政党が、得票率に応じた議会勢力を形成しやすくなる一方で、単独で政権を
掌握するハードルを高める制度となる。実現すれば、選挙で「勝者」を決める政治から、選挙後
の政党間交渉によって「多数派」を形成する政治へと英国政治を変える可能性がある。

■改革の多くは次期総選挙後 
 今回示された政策の多くは、本格的な実施時期がかなり先に設定されている。ＮＣＳの本格導
入は次期議会で、年金トリプルロックの見直しは2030 年以降を想定している。ＥＵとの関係や選
挙制度改革についても、次期総選挙のマニフェストに向けて具体策を検討していく性格が強い。 
 背景にあるのは、バーナム政権が抱える厳しい財政制約がある。2026～27 会計年度に入ってか
ら政府の借入は予算責任局（ＯＢＲ）が３月時点で想定した水準を上回って推移している（詳細
は９月 24 日付けレポート「財政制約に直面する英バーナム政権」を参照されたい） 。歳入は想定
以上に増えているものの、社会保障給付や国債利払いなどを中心に歳出がそれ以上に膨らんでい
るためだ。新政権の政策転換によって歳出が増えたというよりも、既存政策に必要な費用が当初
想定を上回っていることが財政を圧迫している。バーナム政権は、スターマー前政権が2024 年の
総選挙で掲げた公約を今議会中は基本的に引き継ぐ方針を示唆している。このため、増税や歳出
削減によって大規模な財源を捻出する余地も限られる。

■理念から実行力が問われる段階へ 
 今回の労働党大会を通じて、バーナム政権の政策理念はかなり明確になった。公共サービスへ

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
の政府関与を強め、社会保障の配分を見直し、ＥＵとの関係を再構築する。さらに選挙制度を含
めた政治改革にも踏み込む。財政規律と市場との関係を重視したスターマー前政権と比べれば、
政策の重心を政府による積極的な介入と公共サービスの再建に移す姿勢が鮮明になっている。た
だし、こうした改革には大きな財源が必要となる。社会的ケアの拡充だけでなく、住宅、エネル
ギー、水道への公的関与を強めれば、中長期的には政府の財政負担が増える可能性がある。一
方、英国では国債利払い費や社会保障費が増加し、財政余力はむしろ縮小している。 
ここにバーナム政権が抱える最大の課題がある。すなわち、政府の役割を拡大するという政策
の方向性と、政府が自由に使える財源が乏しいという現実をどう両立させるかである。この意味
で、今回の党大会はバーナム政権の「政策転換」を示す大会だったが、その実行可能性を判断す
る材料はまだ十分ではない。次の重要な節目となるのが10 月 28 日に予定される予算案だろう。
党大会で示した改革の方向性を維持しながら、悪化する財政状況にどう対応するのか。歳出削
減、増税、借入増加のいずれにも政治的・経済的な制約があるなか、バーナム政権は具体的な選
択を迫られる。

3
《歐元投資關鍵-歐元區經濟活動加速擴張》
7月以來，北約示警灰色地帶模糊戰的風險升溫，導
致歐元短線偏弱 ，歐洲資金明顯轉向瑞郎進行避險 。
然而，歐洲基本面此時卻持續改善。歐元區9月景氣
加速擴張 ，綜合PMI初值創41個月新高 ，服務業與
製造業同步改善 ，其中製造業產出創 55個月高點。
德國成長動能增強 ，法國則10個月來首度恢復擴張 。
新訂單連續第3個月成長，增速為2022年5月以來最
快，出口訂單亦持續回升，帶動未完成訂單自2022
年6月以來首度增加。另一方面，中東衝突推升能源

價格，使投入成本與售價增速升至近4個月高點。整
體而言，歐元區經濟韌性優於預期 ，但通膨升溫與
企業信心偏弱仍是後續關注焦點。
貨幣政策方面 ，歐洲央行官員持續釋出偏鷹訊號 ，
認為能源價格再度上漲將推升並延長通膨壓力 ，未
來數月歐元區通膨可能接近4%。愛爾蘭央行行長表
示不排除10月再度升息，強調每次會議皆有行動可
能。首席經濟學家 Lane指出第 2輪能源衝擊將帶動
食品與商品價格上漲 。多位官員認為經濟仍具韌性 ，
為進一步收緊政策提供空間 ，市場也預期未來一年
歐洲央行仍有多次升息機會 ，以防止通膨預期根深
蒂固。基本面繼續支持 ECB升息的環境下 ，預計美
歐利差收斂將提供歐元支撐。4Q26目標價1.19。

《英鎊投資關鍵-英國央行維持利率3.75%不變》
9月經濟數據方面，9月PMI連續第3個月擴張，但成
長動能放緩。綜合PMI初值創6月以來新低，製造業
與服務業活動同步降溫 。其中，服務業受內需疲弱
及地緣政治不確定性拖累；製造業則受惠於 AI投資
與國防支出增加 ，但消費需求疲弱仍構成壓力 。通
膨壓力持續升溫，企業投入成本創6月以來最高，並
推升企業售價增幅 。英國經濟維持低速擴張 ，GDP
季增率僅約0.1%，但成本與通膨壓力升高 ，反映經
濟成長疲弱與通膨升溫並存的局面。
貨幣政策方面，9月央行維持利率3.75%不變，為成

熟國家少數維持利率不變的國家，英鎊兌美元創3個
月新低。央行總裁貝里表示 ，能源成本上升對英國
物價和薪資影響有限 ，但若這種波動持續下去 ，未
來有可能需要調高基準利率 。目前消費者物價指數
為3.1%，低於4月央行預測值 ，實際通膨展望低於
預期提供央行維持貨幣政策的基調 。展望後續，歐
非中東地緣政治情勢短期仍處於高不確定性階段 ，
並且導致近期天然氣價格飆升 ，英國仍有升息壓力 。
英國貨幣政策短線持穩 ，而經濟數據面則小幅改善 ，
但財政政策不確定性風險大幅削弱英鎊的投資誘因 ，
維持英鎊中性評等，英鎊兌美元年底目標1.34。

整點關卡的決心。目前日本利率(無擔保隔夜拆款利

Document 401: マーケット見通し『厳選指標』(2026年10月号)

Source PDF: all report pdf/マーケット見通し『厳選指標』(2026年10月号).pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

・   台湾は言わずと知れた、 半導体の生産集積地であり、
今や世界で最も注目すべき経済主体の一つです。 Ａ Ｉ 関
連投資の動向を把握する上で極めて重要な経済指標
があります。
・   それは毎月20日頃に発表される台湾企業の輸出受注
です。 輸出受注全体の ８ 割を占めるのは電子部品や情
報技術製品で、 そこに Ａ Ｉ を動かすために必要な半導体
関連製品が含まれます。 26年 ７ 月は前年比＋61.9％と
驚異的な伸び率でした。 製品価格が急騰し、 受注額を押
し上げた形です。
・   この指標は世界の代表的な半導体銘柄で構成される Ｓ
Ｏ Ｘ 指数と連動性があります。 25年春頃は トランプ関税
等の影響によってかい離が生じましたが、 それ以外の局
面で波形は一致してきました。 半導体銘柄を巡っては、
バブルも指摘されていますが、 この指標をみる限り、 裏
付けを伴った株価上昇と言えるのではないでし ょ うか。
このページでは筆者が注目する指標を四半期に一度解説します。 常に変化する金融市場参加者の関心を踏まえ、 その

時々の重要指標を選定します。
・   日本では 「賃金上昇率は高いほど良い」 といった風潮も
ありますが、 「物価の安定」 を考えた場合、 高すぎる賃金
上昇率は困りものです。 米国では2022年頃に賃金イ
ンフレに悩まされました。 賃金と物価は表裏一体の関
係にありますから、 賃金上昇率が高まりすぎると、 イン
フレを招いてしまいます。
・   労働者個々人で考えた場合、 10％の賃上げは嬉しいの
ですが、 それがマクロ的な現象になってしまえば、 イン
フレは ２ ％で済むはずがありません。 増加した労働コス
トが価格に転嫁されるからです。
・   その点、 最近の米国では賃金上昇率が鈍化しており、 安
心感があります。 雇用統計で示される平均時給は前年
比で ３ ％程度の軌道にあり、 方向感は下向きです。
・   FRBは関税と原油高に由来するインフレ圧力を警戒し
ていますが、 賃金上昇率の鈍化に鑑みると、 インフレの
基調は緩やかに鈍化すると筆者考えています。
資料1 台湾  輸出受注 ・ Ｓ Ｏ Ｘ指数

（出所） 台湾経済部,LSEGにより作成
資料2 米国　平均時給
（出所） ） 米雇用統計より作成
厳選指標 （9月10日時点）
